# Mesa — starters and five more

Read-only evaluation. The first five alternatives add a Type absent from the starter set first, then sort alphabetically; this is a preview, not a stored rank. WSRS-TSO will use actual curated Type coverage. No resource is marked Curated here. Items needing resolution stay in the administrator list.

## Addiction

Combine urgent adult access, local medication and treatment options, adolescent and women-focused care, different mutual-support choices, family help, harm reduction and court-related treatment. Preserve reserve alternatives for access, preference and capacity.

### Starter set

- **Community Bridges, Inc. · Mesa crisis stabilization, withdrawal management and treatment access** — Adult crisis assessment, withdrawal/stabilization and treatment routing with access regardless of ability to pay. Limitation: Adult crisis criteria and clinical triage apply; the entry point does not guarantee a residential bed.

- **New Hope Behavioral Health Center — OTP/SUD Treatment Clinic** — Mesa opioid medication treatment with local intake and public-funding routes. Limitation: Medication, dosing, payer and available funding require assessment; check the specific pregnancy or age-related pathway.

- **Unhooked Recovery - Mesa Detox, Residential, and Outpatient Services** — Local detox, residential and outpatient options with separately labeled intake contacts. Limitation: Different levels of care use different locations and contacts; admission, payment and space must be confirmed.

- **Arizona Women's Recovery Center - Women's Outpatient Treatment and Supportive Housing** — Women-centered outpatient care and treatment-linked housing/family supports. Limitation: Housing is tied to treatment; Phoenix travel, clinical fit, funding and openings matter.

- **Adolescent Substance Abuse Program (ASAP) - Mesa Adolescent IOP** — Mesa adolescent outpatient substance-use treatment with parent involvement. Limitation: For ages 12–18 with parent participation; in-person counselling only, without detox or residential care.

- **Alcoholics Anonymous · East Valley Intergroup** — Free local alcohol-recovery mutual support and helpline. Limitation: Peer support cannot manage dangerous withdrawal or replace clinical treatment.

- **SMART Recovery - Mesa Meetings and Family & Friends Meeting Finder** — Alternative self-management mutual support with online access and family options. Limitation: Use current meeting listings; this is mutual support, not a clinic or crisis service.

- **East Valley Al-Anon** — Local support for family/friends affected by another person's drinking. Limitation: For people affected by another person’s drinking; meeting times and language options vary.

- **Shot in the Dark - Mesa Harm-Reduction Site** — Mesa low-barrier overdose-prevention/harm-reduction supply access. Limitation: Text for current Mesa location details; the street address is intentionally not public.

- **SAGE Counseling, Inc. - City of Mesa Diversion Treatment Program** — Local court/diversion-related substance-use education and outpatient treatment. Limitation: Assessment, court paperwork, costs and payer rules depend on the programme.

Gaps: No starter guarantees an available residential bed, transportation or payment. Pregnancy-specific inpatient care, long-term recovery housing and additional culturally specific pathways remain important reserve choices.

- **Agave Ridge Behavioral Hospital - Medical Detox and Addiction Treatment** — Offers assessed inpatient addiction care and clinically suitable detox for adolescents or adults, with age/program fit and payment confirmed.

- **AHCCCS - How to Apply / Get Covered behavioral-health funding pathway** — Adds a public funding route to explore when substance-use treatment is needed and AHCCCS eligibility is pending or denied.

- **AHCCCS - Substance Use Prevention, Treatment and Recovery Services Block Grant Pathway** — Explains the public treatment-funding pathway for uninsured or underinsured Arizona residents seeking substance-use care.

## Children/Pregnancy

Combine local prenatal care, nutrition, health coverage, child-care assistance, early learning, developmental evaluation, emergency child shelter, diapers, parenting consultation and maternal/family mental-health care. Distinct providers and access routes support choice and spread referrals.

### Starter set

- **Wesley Community & Health Centers - Recker Road Prenatal Care** — Provides bilingual prenatal care in Mesa, with uninsured discounts and help coordinating delivery. Limitation: Delivery is in Phoenix; prenatal transfers should have their first Wesley appointment by 34 weeks, and fees/coverage depend on the family.

- **NATIVE HEALTH WIC - Mesa Clinic** — Adds a local route to pregnancy and young-child nutrition benefits and breastfeeding support. Limitation: WIC eligibility and a nutrition assessment apply; this is not emergency food or a diaper program.

- **AHCCCS - Health Insurance for Children / KidsCare** — Helps families obtain children's health coverage rather than paying for each visit without insurance. Limitation: Eligibility is application-based; KidsCare and ordinary AHCCCS have different rules, and former-foster-young-adult coverage is a separate pathway.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Child Care Assistance** — Starts the application for help paying an approved child-care provider, a major barrier for working families. Limitation: Funding limits and the current waitlist matter; it does not promise immediate child care.

- **Maricopa County Human Services Department - Early Head Start and Head Start** — Adds early learning and family support with a Mesa application office. Limitation: Age, service area and eligibility rules apply, and placement depends on openings.

- **Child Crisis Arizona - Emergency Children's Shelter** — Provides a direct inquiry route when a caregiver temporarily cannot keep a child safely cared for. Limitation: The public emergency shelter is for ages 2–10 and depends on capacity; it is not whole-family housing.

- **A New Leaf / MesaCAN - Arizona Diaper Distribution Pilot Program** — Addresses an immediate recurring expense for families needing diapers or pull-ups. Limitation: Pilot eligibility, funding and stock apply; confirm current access before promising a supply.

- **Southwest Human Development - Birth to Five Helpline** — Provides free bilingual phone/text support for parenting, behavior, feeding and development questions without travel. Limitation: Specialist response has service hours, and it is consultation rather than emergency care or diagnosis.

- **Women's Health Innovations of Arizona - Maternal Mental Health Counseling and Family Therapy** — Adds professional maternal, child and family mental-health care, including nearby offices and telehealth. Limitation: Insurance, self-pay costs and appointments must be checked; it is not emergency crisis care.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Arizona Early Intervention Program Central Referral** — Adds the statewide referral and developmental evaluation route for children under three, complementing general parenting consultation. Limitation: Evaluation determines eligibility and services; referral does not guarantee a therapy or provider, and children three or older use the school-district route.

Gaps: Home visiting and doulas, preschool screening, foster/kinship services, specialized infant care, respite, grief support, child safety equipment, maternity housing and teen-parent programmes remain useful reserve options.

## Clothing/Household

Combine several practical clothing routes across days and nearby locations with work attire, foster-family and broader child supplies, partner-referred furniture, child beds, move-in support and free laundry. Different providers and access models spread referrals while matching distinct needs.

### Starter set

- **Casa de Amor Community Resource Center - Clothing Assistance** — A Mesa clothing option with Monday–Wednesday morning access, spreading ordinary clothing referrals beyond one downtown provider. Limitation: Check current sizes and any intake requirements; published location hours do not guarantee particular items.

- **City Hope Arizona / Christ Church - City Hope Centers Clothing** — Adds no-cost clothing with Saturday availability in nearby Gilbert, useful when weekday-only Mesa options do not fit. Limitation: Clothing is at Gilbert/Phoenix centers; the Mesa center provides food only. Bring government ID; one visit per center monthly, and capacity can close distribution early.

- **Paz de Cristo Community Center - Clothing Closet / Clothe Program** — Adds a downtown Mesa route for adult clothing and appointment-based Deseret Industries clothing vouchers. Limitation: The clothing program does not stock children’s clothing; confirm the relevant closet or voucher schedule.

- **One Small Step / Clothes Cabin - Back to Work** — Addresses a specific employment barrier: uniforms and required work shoes for someone starting a job. Limitation: Bring photo ID and employer/new-hire documentation or the program request form; job-required footwear is conditional, not a general shoe giveaway.

- **Jose's Closet / AZAFAP - Donation-Based Boutique for Foster, Kinship, and Adoptive Families** — Supplies clothing and baby essentials for foster, kinship and adoptive households, including eligible household children through age 21. Limitation: Membership and placement/guardianship/adoption documents apply; monthly clothing/diaper limits and available inventory matter.

- **Child Crisis Arizona - Resource Distribution Center** — Provides a Mesa route for children and families needing several essentials together, including clothing, diapers and toiletries. Limitation: Need/income assessment applies. Call the Resource Distribution Center about referral requirements and available supplies before visiting.

- **Basics for Phoenix - Beds and Basic Furniture** — Adds household beds and furniture through a school, church or social-service partner, beyond clothing-only assistance. Limitation: A verified partner must request help under current criteria; confirm service area, delivery logistics and any costs rather than promising immediate free furniture.

- **Sleep in Heavenly Peace - AZ-Phoenix Chapter / Apply for a Bed** — Offers a child-specific bed application route that a family or referring helper can begin directly. Limitation: Children must be ages 3–17 and within a serving chapter’s area; ZIP screening, suitable space, availability and delivery rules apply. A submitted request is not a promised bed.

- **La Mesa Ministries — Resource Center and housing/document support** — Connects move-in furniture and household items with Mesa housing navigation and a financial-management class. Limitation: The class lasts four weeks; further qualification and funding apply. This is not a same-day furniture pickup program.

- **Social Spin Foundation — Mesa Wash With Care laundry and outreach** — Adds free laundry in Mesa so a household can keep existing clothing usable even when it does not need replacement clothes. Limitation: Mesa WashWithCare is Wednesday 1–4, last check-in3:15, up to two loads per household with detergent; do not substitute donation hours or Phoenix pickup rules.

Gaps: Inventory, sizes and referral rules can limit every route. Additional school-uniform and formalwear choices, medical equipment, appliance replacement, refugee resettlement goods and survivor-specific supplies remain useful reserve or cross-category options. No selected service guarantees same-day whole-house furnishing.

## Disability

A ten-resource starting set balances system entry and care funding with employment, family guidance, equipment, rights, sensory access and a local direct-service provider. Selections reflect complementary client pathways and practical access, not a claim that these providers are superior. Numerous other providers remain available so a curator can choose alternatives that fit a person’s needs and avoid concentrating referrals unnecessarily.

### Starter set

- **Ability360 - Center for Independent Living Services and Information & Referral** — Provides a cross-disability starting point for independent-living questions, local information and help navigating community supports. Limitation: Individual services and eligibility differ; this is not a guarantee of funded personal care or an immediate provider placement.

- **AHCCCS - Arizona Long Term Care System (ALTCS)** — Provides the long-term-care coverage pathway that can make in-home or residential support affordable for qualifying people. Limitation: Financial and medical criteria apply, and evaluation takes time. An application is not an immediate care placement.

- **Encircle Families - Family Support and Parent-to-Parent Disability Navigation** — Gives families no-cost guidance, parent connections and help navigating a child’s disability, school and health systems. Limitation: Program scope and availability vary. Navigation and mentoring do not replace a school evaluation, clinical treatment or legal representation.

- **AzTAP Assistive Technology Services and AzLAT Loans** — Lets people explore assistive technology through assessment, demonstrations, trials, reuse and possible financing. Limitation: Equipment inventory and loan terms vary. AzLAT financing must be repaid; school-only lending has a separate professional request process.

- **Disability Rights Arizona - protection and advocacy legal services** — Provides disability-rights resources and an intake route for legal barriers involving services, education, access or discrimination. Limitation: Cases are selected according to priorities and capacity. The published notice pauses new intake until September 28, 2026 at 8 a.m.; recheck before referral.

- **Foundation for Blind Children · Blind and Low-Vision Services** — Adds specialized vision-loss guidance, training and technology support for children, adults and older adults. Limitation: Confirm the correct program, funding and location. The organization’s name does not mean that only children qualify.

- **Valley Center of the Deaf - Deaf, DeafBlind, and Hard-of-Hearing Independent Living Services** — Adds a practical deaf and DeafBlind service pathway for communication access, independent living and support navigation. Limitation: Eligibility, equipment and interpreting services have separate conditions. Confirm the appropriate program and current communication contact.

- **Copa Health — Disability living, in-home, day and employment supports** — Adds a Mesa provider with concrete day, home, community-living and employment supports after the appropriate authorization is arranged. Limitation: DDD, VR, behavioral-health or other payer authorization and openings vary by program. The administrative location is not a walk-in residential placement.

- **DES Division of Developmental Disabilities — eligibility and member supports** — Adds the eligibility and support-coordination route for developmental-disability services, connecting families to authorized home, day, respite and employment supports. Limitation: DDD eligibility and programme authorization apply; many funded supports also require ALTCS, and neither diagnosis nor enrollment guarantees a particular service.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security · Vocational Rehabilitation (VR)** — Adds an individualized public employment pathway for people whose disability makes finding or keeping work difficult. Limitation: Eligibility and an approved employment plan govern training, equipment and travel support; this is not a general tuition or transportation benefit.

Gaps: The set does not individually cover every diagnosis, SSA cash-benefit application, caregiver respite provider, accessible-transport option, home-modification fund, recreation activity or residential budget. The reserve includes these pathways and additional direct providers; all services remain subject to their own eligibility, authorization and capacity.

## Domestic Violence

Start with three independent shelter providers spread across the Valley, alongside mobile advocacy, trauma care, civil legal help, court access, escape assistance,always-available support and address privacy. This spreads referrals while covering materially different needs. It is a curation starting set, not a claim these providers fit every survivor.

### Starter set

- **Catholic Charities Community Services Arizona - My Sisters' Place Domestic Violence Shelter** — Establish an East Valley shelter with a direct 24-hour hotline, free services and explicit access for individuals, families, men and women. Limitation: Beds require safety/intake screening; the public Catholic Charities office is not the confidential shelter.

- **Chicanos Por La Causa - De Colores Domestic and Sexual Violence Services** — Adds a separate bilingual shelter and community advocacy provider, with direct nonshelter access and pet support. Limitation: Confirm shelter openings, language/animal needs and whether the public community site is the appropriate appointment location.

- **New Life Center - Shelter and Survivor Advocacy** — Adds West Valley shelter capacity through a third provider, plus pet accommodation, mobile support and distinct sexual-assault services. Limitation: Shelter, Sunshine services and Fresh Start outreach have separate contacts/access rules; none guarantees immediate placement.

- **Eve's Place Community Services - Mobile Advocacy Program** — Provides mobile advocacy, court support and practical connections for survivors who are not entering a shelter. Limitation: Arrange a safe contact and meeting; the public mailing suite is not a walk-in service location.

- **La Frontera EMPACT-Suicide Prevention Center - Trauma Healing Services** — Provides free trauma therapy and 24-hour survivor intake, including local sites and Maricopa telehealth, rather than relying only on shelter-based counseling. Limitation: Ask which therapy or mobile service fits and when it is available; hotline availability does not guarantee an immediate appointment.

- **Community Legal Services - Civil Legal Aid and MesaCAN Intake** — Adds civil legal screening for family-law, housing and other survivor-related problems, with potentially broader remedies than protective-order paperwork alone. Limitation: Income, funding, case type and capacity apply; immigration-status exceptions need screening and representation is not automatic.

- **Mesa Municipal Court - Protective Order Filing and Emergency Order Pathway** — Provides an actual local court filing/hearing route after AZPOINT preparation, making the protective-order process actionable. Limitation: Court jurisdiction and pending family cases affect where to file; an order needs judicial review and service, and is not a safety guarantee.

- **Control Alt Delete - One-Time Emergency Escape Assistance** — Adds possible help with immediate escape costs, transportation or temporary arrangements that safety planning alone cannot supply. Limitation: An advocate must refer; survivors cannot self-refer and funding or hotel help is never guaranteed.

- **National Domestic Violence Hotline - 24/7 Confidential Hotline, Chat and Text** — Adds round-the-clock national advocacy and safety planning when local offices are closed or another confidential contact is needed. Limitation: It does not itself supply local shelter beds or financial aid; use a device and contact method the survivor considers safe.

- **Arizona Secretary of State - Address Confidentiality Program** — Adds a specific privacy safeguard: substitute-address use and eligible mail forwarding for survivors moving to a confidential location. Limitation: Eligibility includes moving plans/recent move, documentation and an Application Assistant; the address cannot be used before approval.

Gaps: Keep Deaf/ASL, Native/tribal, LGBTQ-specific, veteran/military, campus, older-adult, child-specific, immigration and longer-term housing alternatives searchable in the reserve. The selected shelter providers have different access and pet arrangements; no bed availability is guaranteed.

## Education

Balances adult learning, language, technology, technical careers, college costs, youth transition, tutoring and consequential access barriers. Uses multiple providers and avoids treating annual closed scholarship applications as immediate funding.

### Starter set

- **Mesa Public Schools Community Education · Mesa Adult Education and Family Literacy** — Provides a local adult-education entry point for foundational skills, English and high-school-equivalency goals. Limitation: Enrollment, testing, lawful-presence rules and current class schedules must be checked for the chosen course.

- **Friendly House · Adult Education and Workforce Development** — Adds a second community adult-learning provider with English and workforce-linked instruction, spreading referrals beyond one school system. Limitation: Travel to Phoenix and in-person orientation/testing may be needed even for hybrid classes.

- **Mesa Public Library — adult learning, computers and digital help** — Provides approachable library learning tools and digital instruction for clients beginning with basic technology or conversation skills. Limitation: Branch schedules vary, and some online products require a library card.

- **Mesa / Maricopa Community Colleges — financial aid and FAFSA help** — Helps clients turn college plans into an application and funding plan rather than assuming a scholarship will cover the bill. Limitation: Aid has program-specific eligibility and deadlines; advice does not guarantee funding.

- **East Valley Institute of Technology — Adult career training and basic education** — Adds a local hands-on technical-training route for people seeking a vocational credential. Limitation: Adult technical programs have separate admission requirements and fees; they are not the free adult-basic-education program.

- **Be A Leader Foundation · College Access Programs** — Supports young people preparing for college with application guidance and transition activities. Limitation: Each program has its own age, school and participation requirements.

- **Mesa Community College - Disability Resources and Services** — Provides the formal route for college disability accommodations, addressing an access barrier other education providers may not resolve. Limitation: Documentation and advance processing are needed; accommodations are determined individually.

- **Maricopa County Library District — Online Tutoring and Test Preparation** — Adds remote subject tutoring, writing and test preparation for learners who cannot rely on an in-person class alone. Limitation: Requires an MCLD account; live tutor subjects and hours vary.

- **U.S. Department of Labor Job Corps - Phoenix Job Corps Center** — Combines diploma completion with career training for eligible young people who need a coordinated pathway. Limitation: Usually ages 16–24 with income, status, background and readiness requirements; intake and residential arrangements require confirmation.

- **Arizona Department of Education — Homeless Education Liaison Finder** — Connects families and unaccompanied youth to the school liaison when housing instability threatens enrollment or attendance. Limitation: A school-support route, not shelter placement; the local liaison assesses the student’s circumstances.

Gaps: Specialized refugee and Native American programs, foster-youth aid, apprenticeships, parent education and business learning remain valuable reserve expansions. Availability, travel, schedules and individual eligibility still govern referrals.

## Employment

Combine three complementary local general-entry providers with disability, youth, reentry, older-worker, women-focused and newcomer access, plus broad local occupational training. This spreads referrals while keeping substantial differences in client fit visible.

### Starter set

- **Workforce Center @ Mesa** — Provides a local starting point for job-search tools, coaching and workforce partner referrals. Limitation: Its self-service hours differ from nearby MesaCAN services; confirm appointments and the specific partner program.

- **The Worker / St. Joseph the Worker - Mesa Employment Services** — Adds practical help getting ready to start work, alongside direct Mesa employment coaching. Limitation: Uniforms, transportation and other material help depend on documentation, program rules and current resources.

- **The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints - Employment Services Mesa Center** — Adds another Mesa coaching and job-search route, available to people of any faith, to spread referrals across providers. Limitation: Confirm center availability and the form of support; it cannot guarantee a job or provide every financial support service.

- **ARIZONA@WORK Maricopa County - Youth Workforce Services, East Valley Career Center** — Covers eligible young people who need education, work preparation or paid experience together. Limitation: In-school and out-of-school routes have different ages and qualifying barriers, and individual opportunities depend on enrollment.

- **Maricopa County Human Services / ARIZONA@WORK - Smart Justice Program** — Adds a focused path for adults with felony histories who meet county reentry-workforce criteria. Limitation: Current supervision or recent-release rules apply; other justice-affected clients may need a different reentry provider.

- **Arizona DES - Senior Community Service Employment Program (SCSEP) / Mature Worker Program** — Covers eligible older adults needing paid community-service experience while preparing for regular employment. Limitation: Age 55+, unemployment, income and other federal eligibility rules apply; placements and local provider capacity are limited.

- **Mesa Community College · Career and Technical Education Programs** — Offers a broad local training route across occupations instead of assuming every client needs immediate job placement. Limitation: Tuition, equipment, prerequisites and program admission vary; public-college enrollment does not guarantee aid or a job.

- **Fresh Start Women's Foundation - Career Training, Coaching, and Employment Advising** — Adds women-focused career advice and training pathways with related support for economic independence. Limitation: Programs have separate enrollment requirements; children cannot accompany participants to classes or appointments.

- **International Rescue Committee in Phoenix — newcomer case support and employment** — Provides refugee and humanitarian-newcomer employment help integrated with language, education and financial support. Limitation: Status and time-since-arrival rules vary by program; confirm the current Glendale intake and available services.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security · Vocational Rehabilitation (VR)** — Adds individualized vocational rehabilitation for people whose disability is a barrier to obtaining or keeping work. Limitation: Eligibility and an approved employment plan govern training, equipment and other supports; referral does not guarantee funding or placement.

Gaps: These ten do not promise an immediate vacancy, cover every trade, or replace specialized veteran and blind/low-vision providers. Staffing, app shifts, construction apprenticeships, work clothing, child-care help and housing-linked programs remain available in the reserve. Some specialized providers share public funding systems, so referral fit and current capacity still matter.

## Financial Assistance

Combine local emergency help, recurring public benefits, income after job loss, financial counseling, tax preparation, benefit appeals, medical-bill relief and two consequential specialist pathways. Several public benefits necessarily share a government administrator; other options spread referrals across distinct community providers.

### Starter set

- **A New Leaf / MesaCAN - Rent, Mortgage, and Utility Assistance** — Provides Mesa’s local rent, mortgage and utility screening route, with separate instructions for each need. Limitation: Appointments and funds are limited; rental requests require the published first-Thursday phone window.

- **Health-e-Arizona Plus — benefits applications, notices and records** — Provides one online route to screen or apply for nutrition, cash and health benefits, with community-assistor options. Limitation: Each benefit has separate eligibility and document rules; an application is not approval or immediate money.

- **Social Security Administration - SSI/SSDI Disability Income Application Pathway** — Adds the direct federal disability-income pathway, covering both SSI and SSDI without confusing their rules. Limitation: Income, disability and insured-work requirements differ; decisions and appeals can take time.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Unemployment Insurance Benefits** — Adds wage replacement for eligible people who have recently lost work. Limitation: Applicants must meet UI rules and continue required claims and work-search reporting.

- **GreenPath Financial Wellness - Free Credit, Debt and Housing Counseling** — Offers remote debt and budget counseling when a client needs a manageable financial plan. Limitation: Counseling is not a cash grant; optional plans or specialized services may have fees.

- **Azura / Mesa United Way - Volunteer Income Tax Assistance (VITA)** — Adds free local tax preparation that can help eligible households claim refunds and credits. Limitation: Schedules and return scope vary; confirm the currently published summer or next-season arrangement.

- **Community Legal Services - Public Benefits Legal Assistance** — Adds legal help when a public benefit has been denied, reduced or terminated. Limitation: Intake, financial eligibility, legal scope and representation capacity apply.

- **Banner Health - Arizona Financial Assistance Program for Discounted and Charity Care** — Adds a medical-bill relief pathway that can matter even when ordinary rent or utility programs cannot help. Limitation: It applies to eligible Banner bills; the applicable policy and financial review determine any reduction.

- **Disabled American Veterans Chapter 8 - Veteran Financial Assistance Grant** — Adds a local veteran emergency grant with concrete Mesa intake and small covered-expense limits. Limitation: The grant is limited to eligible veterans, up to $300, with proof that any remaining balance can be paid.

- **Control Alt Delete - One-Time Emergency Escape Assistance** — Adds an advocate-referred route for immediate practical barriers to escaping domestic violence. Limitation: A qualified advocate must request help; survivors cannot self-apply and assistance depends on funds and scope.

Gaps: The set does not cover every circumstance: caregiver costs, refugee-specific aid, matched savings, funeral costs, home or vehicle repairs, disease-specific funds and city/provider-specific bill discounts remain in the explained reserve. Geographic and eligibility exclusions mean no single resource fits every client.

## Reentry Support

Nine distinct providers cover local clinical and employment access, record barriers, men’s and women’s housing, veteran and youth pathways, recovery treatment and practical-expense help for a group often excluded elsewhere. Different providers and eligibility routes spread referrals; more specialized alternatives remain in the reserve.

### Starter set

- **The Stone Community Health Center - Reentry Support Program** — Provides Mesa reentry counseling and case management with self-referral and possible virtual access. Limitation: Confirm coverage, costs and appointment availability; this is not immediate housing.

- **Workforce Center @ Mesa** — Provides local workforce help for jobseekers returning to employment, with access beyond a felony-only program. Limitation: Office hours and appointment arrangements vary by day; employment is not guaranteed.

- **Community Legal Services - Set Aside and Reentry Legal Assistance** — Adds legal screening and representation options for eligible people facing criminal-record barriers. Limitation: Legal-aid eligibility, case scope and capacity apply; record relief is not automatic.

- **Bridges Reentry — Magdalene residential and mentoring programs** — Adds a women-focused residential and mentoring pathway extending beyond short-term release planning. Limitation: The program has limited beds; confirm eligibility, costs, openings and placement arrangements.

- **The Mesa House Inc. - Safe, clean re-entry program for men** — Adds a Mesa transitional-housing option for men, including veterans, with initial work-transport support. Limitation: Confirm current costs, length of stay, vacancy and house rules; it is not an emergency shelter.

- **Phoenix VA Health Care System - Health Care for Re-entry Veterans and Veterans Justice Outreach** — Connects justice-involved veterans with VA reentry outreach and eligible health-care services. Limitation: VA eligibility and criminal-justice circumstances determine the route; the VA does not replace legal representation.

- **Clarvida - Youth and Family Support Program (YFSP) Arizona** — Adds youth and family support for justice-involved young people in Maricopa County. Limitation: Use the provider service finder to confirm the referral authority, availability and any costs.

- **New Freedom · Core Re-Entry Program and Outpatient Transition Services** — Adds structured recovery treatment and distinct post-release outpatient options under one established provider identity. Limitation: Program-specific coverage, referral and offense restrictions apply; core and outpatient intake are different.

- **AZRSOL — New Directions Reentry Stipend** — Adds a small practical-expense grant for qualifying recently released Arizonans with sexual-offense convictions. Limitation: Current rules limit release timing, income and qualifying incarceration; funding is capped and approval is not guaranteed.

Gaps: No guaranteed same-day housing or universal offense acceptance. Standalone ID/document and transit assistance, additional family reunification services and specialized supervision/treatment remain important reserve or cross-category needs. Women’s housing is capacity-limited; treatment can have diagnosis/coverage gates, and some custody services require an assigned professional.

## Food

Ten providers/programs balance ongoing benefits, infant and senior nutrition, daytime/evening meals, local weekday/weekend groceries and delivery. Different locations and schedules spread referral demand while the reserve retains additional providers and specialized access.

### Starter set

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Nutrition Assistance (SNAP / NA) and Health-e-Arizona Plus** — Provides ongoing grocery benefits and an application route for eligible households. Limitation: Eligibility and processing time apply; expedited benefits are not a same-day food-box promise.

- **Paz de Cristo Community Center - Meals, Basic Needs and Empowerment** — Provides a Mesa evening meal plus separately scheduled emergency food boxes. Limitation: Confirm current meal hours; food-box documents and schedules do not apply to the nightly meal.

- **Society of St. Vincent de Paul - Mesa Dining Room** — Provides a daytime Mesa meal without requiring shelter enrollment. Limitation: Confirm the current meal window; heat-relief hours are seasonal and this is not an overnight shelter.

- **New Hope Community Center - Food Box and Client-Choice Pantry** — Provides an East Mesa choice pantry alongside clothing and practical assistance. Limitation: Food service is ZIP-restricted and has registration cutoffs; other assistance has separate limits.

- **Desert Manna Food Pantry - Saturday Grocery Box Distribution** — Adds a far-east Mesa Saturday grocery option, including pickup by a family member or neighbor. Limitation: Confirm supplies and any documentation; preferred local boundaries apply and the pantry does not deliver.

- **NATIVE HEALTH Mesa — food pantry, nutrition programmes and WIC** — Adds weekday Mesa pantry access with distinct child-snack and senior-food pathways. Limitation: Confirm the Building B schedule and program-specific requirements; nearby WIC uses different intake.

- **Maricopa County Department of Public Health - WIC** — Provides WIC benefits and infant/young-child nutrition support through county clinics. Limitation: Income, age or pregnancy/postpartum rules apply; appointments and clinic schedules differ.

- **Aster Aging — senior-center meals and Meals on Wheels** — Adds local senior meals and home-delivery screening, with dietary options. Limitation: Confirm service area, assessment, costs/subsidies and delivery capacity; lunch donations are not a universal fee.

- **St. Mary's Food Bank / AZ Food Help - Food Finder, Mobile Pantry and Home Delivery** — Adds grocery delivery screening for people facing disability, illness or transportation barriers. Limitation: Delivery has income and access criteria, capacity limits and recipient-presence/ID requirements.

- **United Food Bank - Neighbor's Pantry Mesa Distribution** — Adds a central Mesa choice distribution with an additional SNAP-assistance contact. Limitation: The pantry meets on designated Fridays; confirm the date and do not assume daily food at UFB offices.

Gaps: No guaranteed same-day supply, universal delivery, or universal dietary accommodation. Non-driving clients, people lacking ID, students, tribal households and clients outside particular boundaries may need reserve options. Seasonal programs and resident-only meals need individual checking.

## Medical, Dental, Vision

Ten complementary routes balance general and pediatric primary care, preventive and restorative dentistry, basic vision access, public health, insurance navigation and specialty rehabilitation, with several organizations sharing referrals.

### Starter set

- **Adelante Healthcare - Mesa** — Provides a broad Mesa medical and dental entry point with uninsured assistance and extended clinical hours. Limitation: Fees, coverage and service-specific schedules apply; not every visit is free.

- **Valleywise Community Health Center - Mesa** — Adds a second Mesa safety-net medical home with women’s health, pharmacy and behavioral support, spreading referrals. Limitation: Dental and specialist eye care require another Valleywise site.

- **Banner Children's Community Clinic - East Valley / Mesa** — Provides a no-cost primary-care route for uninsured children through high-school age. Limitation: Does not provide dental, hospital, reproductive or mental-health treatment.

- **Carrington College Mesa Dental Hygiene Clinic** — Provides complimentary preventive dental hygiene in Mesa. Limitation: Teaching visits take time and do not replace restorative or emergency dentistry.

- **ATSU-ASDOH Dental Clinic - Mesa Campus** — Adds comprehensive reduced-fee dental treatment beyond preventive cleanings. Limitation: Treatment is not free and may require screening and multiple teaching visits.

- **Arizona Lions Vision & Hearing Foundation · Vision and Hearing Assistance** — Adds a charitable eye-exam and eyeglass inquiry for people facing cost barriers. Limitation: Confirm current fees, documents and Phoenix appointment availability before travel.

- **Maricopa County Department of Public Health - STI & HIV Clinic, Mesa, with Same-Day PrEP Navigation** — Provides a Mesa public-health route for STI/HIV testing and selected STI treatment. Limitation: The visit fee and appointment capacity apply; HIV treatment and general primary care are outside scope.

- **Maricopa County Department of Public Health - East Valley Immunization Clinic** — Provides local immunizations with a public-health access route. Limitation: Adult vaccine eligibility and stock vary; dated clinic closures need checking.

- **Cover Arizona - Insurance Navigator Program** — Helps clients obtain ongoing insurance coverage through free application assistance. Limitation: An assister cannot guarantee coverage eligibility or immediate approval.

- **GateWay Community College - Healthcare United at GateWay (HUG) Clinic** — Adds free specialty rehabilitation and ultrasound services that ordinary primary-care clinics may not provide. Limitation: Phoenix travel, academic-term capacity, visit limits and service suitability apply.

Gaps: No set of ten covers cancer, HIV treatment, cataract surgery, homebound care, complex dentistry, all prescriptions or every uninsured condition. Reserve search retains those narrower routes. Same-day availability and comprehensive free adult dental treatment are not guaranteed.

## Homeless Services

Ten complementary routes cover local outreach, family and adult shelter assessment, daytime survival needs, youth and veteran pathways, single-women shelter and medical respite, spreading referrals among several agencies.

### Starter set

- **City of Mesa Office of Homeless Solutions - Homeless Resource Line (HOPE Line)** — Provides a Mesa outreach entry point for people living outside and those needing help reaching shelter. Limitation: Outreach response and shelter placement are not immediate guarantees.

- **Family Housing Hub - Coordinated Entry for Families Experiencing Homelessness** — Provides the coordinated-entry route for families with children, including a directed Mesa assessment option. Limitation: Assessment does not guarantee a family shelter vacancy; use the current 211 intake route.

- **A New Leaf - East Valley Men's Center** — Adds an emergency shelter pathway for adult men in Mesa. Limitation: Prescreening and capacity apply; children cannot stay on site.

- **Christ Church / City Hope Arizona - Mesa Day Respite Center and City Hope Mesa Center** — Adds a local daytime place to cool down and connect with practical support. Limitation: Seasonal hours and individual services vary; it is not overnight shelter.

- **Paz de Cristo Community Center - Meals, Basic Needs and Empowerment** — Provides daily meals and scheduled hygiene, mail and document help in downtown Mesa. Limitation: Different services have different schedules and limits; it is not a shelter.

- **Keys to Change - Brian Garcia Welcome Center / Key Campus Single-Adult Coordinated Entry** — Adds the Phoenix campus entry point for single-adult assessment and connected services. Limitation: Travel may be needed; each partner has its own hours and eligibility.

- **Native American Connections - HomeBase Youth Emergency Shelter and Youth Housing Services** — Adds dedicated young-adult shelter and drop-in access, with a wider age range for youth housing assessment. Limitation: Shelter ages 18-24 differ from assessment ages 18-26, and beds require screening.

- **Phoenix VA Health Care System - Community Resource and Referral Center (CRRC) / Homeless Veteran Care** — Provides a federal veteran-specific assessment and housing-support route. Limitation: Veteran and programme eligibility apply; clinical and housing benefits are not automatic.

- **UMOM New Day Centers - Halle Women's Center** — Adds a distinct shelter programme for single women. Limitation: Intake, referral and current capacity need confirmation; this is separate from family housing assessment.

- **Circle the City - Medical Respite, Mobile Medical Care and Street Medicine** — Adds medical outreach and recuperative care for people whose homelessness is complicated by health needs. Limitation: Respite admission depends on clinical criteria and referral; it is not ordinary walk-up shelter.

Gaps: Dedicated DV escape, pets, legal barriers, transitional family housing, employment-linked housing and specialized tribal or HIV support remain important reserves. No starter promises an available bed, immediate placement or universally eligible housing assistance.

## Housing

Balance urgent shelter access, local prevention, longer-term rental options and several important eligibility pathways across different providers. Specialized reserves broaden the directory without requiring curation of every lead.

### Starter set

- **A New Leaf / MesaCAN - Rent, Mortgage, and Utility Assistance** — Provides a local route for rent or mortgage assistance when a Mesa household is at risk of losing its home. Limitation: Income and crisis rules apply; rental appointment calls use a limited monthly window.

- **City of Mesa Housing Authority - HCV, Deposit Assistance, PBV, FUP, HUD-VASH, and Rental Assistance** — Explains Mesa vouchers, deposits and specialized housing-referral options in one local agency entry. Limitation: Each programme has separate rules; the general voucher waitlist is closed and this is not immediate housing.

- **Family Housing Hub - Coordinated Entry for Families Experiencing Homelessness** — Gives families a single coordinated starting point for emergency shelter. Limitation: Screening is not a bed reservation; use 211 first and the Mesa satellite only as directed.

- **Keys to Change - Brian Garcia Welcome Center / Key Campus Single-Adult Coordinated Entry** — Gives single adults a coordinated route into the regional shelter and housing system. Limitation: The assessment location is in Phoenix; opening hours and bed availability must be checked.

- **Sojourner Center Emergency Shelter, Transitional Housing and Community Outreach** — Provides confidential domestic-violence shelter, transitional housing and help with pets. Limitation: Survivor eligibility and safe intake arrangements apply; never send a client to a confidential residence address.

- **House of Refuge — family transitional housing and resident supports** — Adds transitional housing for families who need time and support to reach independent housing. Limitation: The programme has minimum-income and move-in cost requirements; it is not a same-day free shelter.

- **Accessible Space, Inc. - R.J. Piltz Vista Bonita Apartments** — Adds an accessible Mesa apartment option designed for adults with qualifying disabilities. Limitation: Income and disability rules apply, and vacancies or waitlist access must be confirmed.

- **U.S.VETS Phoenix - Housing, SSVF, Mental Health and Workforce Development** — Connects veterans with housing outreach, transitional housing and homelessness prevention. Limitation: Veteran, discharge and income rules depend on the programme; dedicated SSVF and outreach contacts differ.

- **Oxford House - Mesa recovery residences** — Adds self-run sober-living options through a current house-vacancy and application finder. Limitation: Residents pay house expenses and follow recovery rules; each house controls admission.

- **HousingSearch.AZ.gov — free rental-housing search** — Broadens the search to affordable, accessible and market-rate rentals when individual properties do not fit. Limitation: Listings are leads to landlords, not a subsidy or a promise of an available apartment.

Gaps: This set does not cover every senior, foster-youth, reentry, medical-respite or home-modification need. Reserve options address these needs; no listed shelter or property promises a vacancy. Travel ability and programme eligibility must be considered.

## ID Recovery

Combine distinct local help with official issuers, record correction, immigration/consular needs and an interstate record finder. Multiple assistance providers spread referrals and offer alternatives when schedules or eligibility do not fit.

### Starter set

- **Homeless ID Project - Mesa and Phoenix replacement ID services** — Provides specialized help rebuilding missing identity documents, with a scheduled Mesa clinic and a broader Phoenix network. Limitation: Clinic schedules, document requirements and available funding apply; it does not issue government credentials.

- **Paz de Cristo Community Center - Meals, Basic Needs and Empowerment** — Adds a separate Mesa provider for adult ID vouchers, birth-certificate paperwork and receiving mail. Limitation: Tuesday vouchers are limited; birth-certificate assistance requires a hard-copy state ID.

- **Arizona MVD — ID cards, replacements and optional tribal designation** — Provides the official Arizona ID and replacement-credential process, including applicable fee exceptions. Limitation: Applicants still need the required identity and residency evidence; homelessness alone does not create a general fee waiver.

- **Maricopa County Department of Public Health - Office of Vital Registration East Valley office** — Provides the local official route to certified Arizona birth and death records. Limitation: Restricted-record eligibility applies; notarization does not guarantee service without acceptable identity evidence.

- **Social Security Administration - replacement Social Security card service** — Provides the official free Social Security replacement-card route. Limitation: Proof and appointment/card-center rules apply; cards are mailed after a completed request.

- **Maricopa County Law Library Resource Center - Southeast Justice Center** — Helps resolve name-change and record-correction barriers with court forms and procedural support. Limitation: This is legal information, not guaranteed attorney representation; issuing agencies must be updated separately.

- **Immigrant Hope - Mesa low-cost immigration legal aid clinic** — Provides a Mesa-based low-cost legal clinic for eligible immigration-document matters. Limitation: Confirm the specific form and fee; the clinic does not handle asylum or immigration-court cases.

- **Consulate General of Mexico in Phoenix - Mexican identity documents and civil registry records** — Adds Mexican passports, consular identification and civil records through the Phoenix consulate. Limitation: Mexican nationality and service-specific proof, appointment and fee rules apply.

- **VoteRiders Helpline - Arizona ID document assistance** — Adds remote help assembling voter-ID documents and covering eligible document or travel costs. Limitation: Voter-ID purpose controls program fit; ask about a need unrelated to voting.

- **CDC — Where to Write for Vital Records** — Finds the correct issuing office when a needed birth or other vital record comes from outside Arizona. Limitation: The CDC directory does not issue records or pay fees; the selected jurisdiction sets its requirements.

Gaps: Tribal, military, school, foster-care, reentry, survivor and gender-affirming needs are covered in the reserve. No starter is a universal solution for a person with no documents at all.

## Immigration

Combine several independent legal providers with detention and non-detained asylum pathways, local newcomer support, education, benefits and health access. This spreads referrals while covering materially different needs.

### Starter set

- **Immigrant Hope - Mesa low-cost immigration legal aid clinic** — Provides a Mesa-based low-cost clinic for supported immigration applications. Limitation: Initial consultation is $30; further service and USCIS fees may apply. No asylum or immigration-court cases.

- **Catholic Charities Community Services Arizona - Familias Unidas Immigration Legal Services** — Adds a broader immigration legal program, including screening for asylum and removal-defense needs. Limitation: Case acceptance, fees and appointment capacity require individual screening.

- **IRC Phoenix — Immigration, Civics and English/Education Services** — Adds another legal and citizenship route connected with refugee resettlement and integration services. Limitation: Different IRC programs have different eligibility; confirm which intake fits the client.

- **Florence Immigrant & Refugee Rights Project - Legal and social services for detained adults and unaccompanied children** — Covers detention-related legal needs and specialized support for unaccompanied children. Limitation: Representation is limited; adult services focus on detention, and child-program rules differ.

- **Arizona Refugee Center - Refugee and immigrant case management, housing, employment, education and benefits support** — Provides a Mesa newcomer-support contact for practical settlement, education and benefits needs. Limitation: Confirm the current service, appointment and referral process; it is not a substitute for qualified legal representation.

- **Matthew House AZ - East Valley refugee and immigrant integration support** — Adds an East Valley organization connecting newcomer families with English, school, employment and household support. Limitation: Services depend on program fit and capacity; the Chandler mailing address is not a walk-in service location.

- **Mesa Public Schools Community Education · Mesa Adult Education and Family Literacy** — Provides Mesa-based English, civics, citizenship-test and adult-education enrollment guidance. Limitation: Program eligibility, fees and class schedules apply; callers outside stated status criteria should ask about other options.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Arizona Refugee Resettlement Program** — Provides the official Arizona route to refugee cash, medical and contracted resettlement assistance. Limitation: Status, entry or eligibility dates and benefit-specific rules apply; not every immigrant qualifies.

- **Wesley Health Center at Recker Road — Mesa clinic and refugee-care access** — Connects newcomer patients with health care and language-access support, including a Mesa clinic. Limitation: Call the refugee-care line to confirm the appropriate clinic, service and payment arrangements.

- **Asylum Program of Arizona** — Adds an Arizona legal-assistance pathway specifically for non-detained asylum seekers. Limitation: Cases are screened for program fit and available funding; assistance may involve shared or reduced attorney costs.

Gaps: Specialized LGBTQ, trafficking, consular, fee-waiver, child-advocate and complaint options remain in the reserve. Representation and current capacity are never guaranteed; urgent deadlines may require more than one referral.

## Legal

Combine several independent providers with broad civil advice, urgent eviction help, protective-order advocacy, local court navigation, immigration services, senior legal aid, child-safety representation, reduced-fee counsel and disability rights. Multiple providers spread referrals and provide alternatives when eligibility or capacity differs.

### Starter set

- **Community Legal Services - Civil Legal Aid and MesaCAN Intake** — Provides broad civil legal-aid screening, including housing, benefits, consumer and family-safety issues, with a Mesa satellite intake. Limitation: Income, case type, conflicts and capacity apply; an online application must be completed by phone.

- **Arizona Legal Center · Free legal consultations and limited legal advocacy** — Adds free consultation and triage for several common legal problems when a client needs an initial assessment. Limitation: A consultation does not promise full representation; the center excludes felonies, public benefits and several other practice areas.

- **Maricopa County / Community Legal Services - Legal Assistance for Renters** — Provides a dedicated eviction-law contact for Mesa tenants facing nonpayment proceedings. Limitation: Legal help does not pay rent; case acceptance and urgent court deadlines require prompt screening.

- **A New Leaf Domestic Violence Court Advocacy** — Adds practical protective-order paperwork and safety-planning support through court advocates. Limitation: Advocates are not attorneys; confirm the appropriate court location and current availability.

- **Maricopa County Law Library Resource Center - Southeast Justice Center** — Provides nearby court forms, procedural guidance and checks for missing information, plus remote assistance. Limitation: Court staff cannot give legal advice or decide which legal strategy a client should choose.

- **Immigrant Hope - Mesa low-cost immigration legal aid clinic** — Provides a Mesa-based low-cost immigration clinic for supported applications. Limitation: The initial consultation is $30; it does not handle asylum or immigration-court cases.

- **Arizona Senior Citizens Law Project · Free legal assistance for Maricopa County seniors** — Adds free civil legal assistance for Maricopa County residents age 60 and older without an income cap. Limitation: New phone requests are taken Tuesdays, 10 a.m.–1 p.m.; accepted cases depend on priorities and capacity.

- **Defenders of Children - Legal Services for Children, Survivors and Protective Caregivers** — Adds attorney-led help for child safety, protective caregivers and related family or survivor legal problems. Limitation: Cases must fit the organization’s expertise; screening does not guarantee representation.

- **State Bar of Arizona and Arizona Foundation for Legal Services and Education - Modest Means Project** — Provides a reduced-fee route when free legal aid does not fit and standard private rates are unaffordable. Limitation: The consultation is $75 for one hour; any additional accepted work is $75 per hour and must be agreed with the lawyer.

- **Disability Rights Arizona - protection and advocacy legal services** — Adds specialized disability-rights advocacy and possible representation. Limitation: Case priorities and capacity apply; the published notice pauses new intake until September 28, 2026 at 8 a.m. Recheck the notice, and use another concurrent route for urgent deadlines.

Gaps: Criminal defense, record relief, bankruptcy, probate, employment enforcement, specialized immigration/removal cases, mediation and tribal or veteran-specific help remain available in the reserve. The starter set cannot guarantee an attorney or a response before a deadline.

## Mental Health

Combine local urgent care and mobile crisis access with affordable continuing treatment, serious-mental-illness care, family and peer support, perinatal help, trauma care and early psychosis. Spread referrals across independent providers while retaining distinctive public-system programs.

### Starter set

- **MIND 24-7 Mesa - Walk-In Urgent Mental Health and Crisis Care** — Offers a local 24-hour walk-in assessment route when waiting for a scheduled appointment is impractical. Limitation: Express Care serves all ages; crisis stabilization is for children and teens. Ongoing care may require another provider.

- **Solari Crisis Response Network - Central Arizona Crisis Line and Mobile Crisis Dispatch** — Connects Arizona callers with crisis assessment and mobile-response dispatch across Mesa. Limitation: Telephone triage determines the response; it does not guarantee transport, immediate arrival or a treatment bed.

- **Valleywise Behavioral Health Center — Mesa and Outpatient Psychiatry** — Provides public-system outpatient psychiatry and therapy with clearly published low appointment fees. Limitation: Appointments only on Monday, Tuesday, Thursday and Friday; this outpatient clinic does not accept insurance.

- **Jewish Family & Children's Service - East Valley Healthcare Center** — Combines child, adult and family treatment with integrated healthcare and sliding-fee access. Limitation: The East Valley center is in Gilbert; confirm program, payer and available appointment before travel.

- **Chicanos Por La Causa - CPLC Centro Esperanza** — Adds a Mesa psychiatric-care and case-management pathway focused on serious mental illness. Limitation: Confirm program eligibility, clinic assignment and payer fit; this is not a crisis receiving center.

- **M.I.K.I.D. - Family Support and Behavioral Health Navigation** — Helps parents navigate children’s care teams, school plans and behavioral-health systems through family-led support. Limitation: Peer navigation complements treatment; it does not prescribe medication or replace a treating clinician.

- **NAMI Valley of the Sun - Peer and Family Mental-Health Support Groups and Education** — Provides free peer and family support beyond formal treatment and appointments. Limitation: Group schedules and locations change; groups are not crisis care or individual therapy.

- **Perinatal Mental Health Alliance of Arizona and PSI HelpLine** — Offers a focused pregnancy/postpartum support route with English and Spanish help and provider connections. Limitation: The PSI HelpLine is a callback service, not an emergency line; immediate maternal counseling has a separate national number.

- **La Frontera EMPACT-Suicide Prevention Center - Trauma Healing Services** — Adds trauma treatment and coordinated support for eligible crime and violence survivors. Limitation: Confirm the intake and appointment site; embedded advocacy-center care is not a general walk-in clinic.

- **Valleywise Health - Mesa First Episode Center** — Provides a Mesa early-psychosis pathway for adolescents and young adults whose needs may be missed by general counseling. Limitation: Ages 15–25; the program screens clinical eligibility and capacity. It is distinct from the Brown Road clinic.

Gaps: ACT-intensive continuing care, student/employee-only services, veteran and tribal-specific care, specialist psychological testing and additional affirming/private practices are available in the reserve. No set guarantees immediate appointments or every insurer.

## Miscellaneous

Combine broad navigation, affordable learning, pet support, culturally relevant community connection and military recreation. Providers and participation routes differ, reducing reliance on one organisation.

### Starter set

- **AllThrive 365 — Resource Navigator** — Provides an accessible first contact for finding aging, caregiving and related services. Limitation: Navigation does not approve benefits or guarantee another provider’s availability.

- **Mesa Arts Center - Creative Aging Lifelong Learning** — Provides free local arts participation for older adults. Limitation: Age 55 minimum; classes and registration change.

- **Mesa Community College - New Frontiers for Lifelong Learning** — Offers a wider mix of peer-led learning, social activities and volunteering. Limitation: Membership and term fees apply; personal care, supervision and transport are not provided.

- **The Arizona Pet Project - Veteran Pet Care and Shelter-Intervention Support** — Covers pet-related barriers that could otherwise disrupt housing or safety. Limitation: Assistance depends on the situation and capacity; this is not stray-animal intake.

- **AZALEA — LGBTQ2S+ inclusion and community connection** — Creates a welcoming community option for LGBTQ2S+ older people and allies. Limitation: This is community participation and advocacy, not clinical or personal care.

- **Phoenix Indian Center - Young Elder Program / Elder Gatherings** — Provides culturally rooted connection and a meaningful mentoring role for Indigenous elders. Limitation: Mentor participation requires age 55+, screening and a monthly time commitment.

- **Be Connected - Coaching Into Connection and Care Transition** — Helps military households find suitable social activities and address participation barriers. Limitation: Military-connected scope and ordinary office hours; not emergency response.

- **Mesa Arts Center - Arts in Service** — Provides a practical arts activity designed for veterans and military-connected participants. Limitation: Class schedules, registration and space vary; it is not therapy.

- **Valley of the Sun YMCA - Military Outreach and Discounted Military Membership** — Adds fitness and family recreation through YMCA military membership programmes. Limitation: Free membership has narrow qualifying groups; other veterans may receive only a discount.

Gaps: General all-age recreation, affordable childcare during activities, and transport to events are not comprehensively covered. Clients may also need categories such as Children/Pregnancy, Disability or Transportation.

## Seniors

Start with food and social contact, help at home, legal protection, dementia and caregiver support, long-term-care coverage, benefits enrollment, rides, Medicare counseling and protective reporting. Several independent organizations share these roles; Aster’s meal, benefit-enrollment and volunteer-ride programmes have distinct practical purposes and access rules.

### Starter set

- **Aster Aging — senior-center meals and Meals on Wheels** — Combines two Mesa senior centers with meal delivery, providing food and regular social contact through one familiar local agency. Limitation: Center meals and home-delivery tracks have different enrollment, cost and capacity rules; specify the appropriate site or track.

- **Maricopa County Human Services Department - Senior & Adult Independent Living Program (SAIL)** — Provides county case management to arrange practical help that can keep an older adult safely at home. Limitation: Eligibility and an in-home assessment apply, and funded services can have a waitlist.

- **Arizona Senior Citizens Law Project · Free legal assistance for Maricopa County seniors** — Provides civil legal help specifically for adults 60 and older, without an income cutoff. Limitation: Maricopa County residency, case scope and capacity apply; new telephone requests are taken Tuesdays, 10 a.m.–1 p.m.

- **Alzheimer's Association - Arizona Chapter Support Groups and 24/7 Helpline** — Offers dementia information and caregiver support around the clock, plus free support groups. Limitation: Groups may require screening; the helpline supports families but does not diagnose or treat dementia.

- **Glory Days - Nonprofit Social Adult Day Program** — Adds a Mesa social adult-day option that gives people with memory loss structured activities and caregivers a break. Limitation: Confirm safe fit, enrollment and fees; this is a social program rather than medical day-health care.

- **AHCCCS - Arizona Long Term Care System (ALTCS)** — Opens the route to Medicaid-funded long-term care when ordinary benefits cannot cover substantial ongoing support. Limitation: Both financial and functional eligibility apply; enrollment does not guarantee a particular provider, placement or number of care hours.

- **Aster Aging - Benefits Enrollment Center and Outreach/Social Services** — Helps older adults complete benefit applications, linking food, health-care and utility needs in one local appointment. Limitation: Aster helps with applications; each benefit agency makes its own eligibility and funding decision.

- **Aster Aging — Neighbors volunteer support** — Adds local volunteer transportation for older adults who need help reaching medical visits, groceries or a pharmacy. Limitation: Enrollment and volunteer capacity apply in Mesa, Chandler and Gilbert; this is not emergency or on-demand transportation.

- **Arizona SHIP - Medicare Counseling and Senior Medicare Patrol** — Adds free, objective Medicare plan and cost-saving counseling, including help with suspected Medicare fraud. Limitation: Counselors do not sell plans or guarantee coverage, provider participation or financial-assistance approval; appointments and benefit eligibility vary.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - Adult Protective Services** — Provides the statewide reporting route for suspected abuse, neglect, self-neglect or exploitation of a vulnerable adult. Limitation: APS is protective investigation, not emergency shelter or confidential survivor advocacy; immediate danger requires 911.

Gaps: Home repairs and safety visits, sensory-disability services, active learning, paid home care, tribal or veteran-specific programs, tax help and employment remain in the reserve. This set does not guarantee same-day care, transportation or funding.

## Transportation

Cover everyday transit, disability access, covered and charitable medical rides, flexible ride assistance, local volunteer trips, bicycles, employment travel, vehicle repairs and long-distance care. Distinct nonprofit and public pathways spread referrals beyond a few providers.

### Starter set

- **Valley Metro — Bus, rail, trip planning and fare access** — Provides the main bus and rail network for everyday Mesa trips, with route planning and cash or digital fare options. Limitation: Route coverage, operating times, transfers and fares need to fit the particular trip; not every address has nearby service.

- **Valley Metro - ADA Paratransit / East Valley Paratransit** — Provides accessible shared rides when a disability prevents someone from using regular buses or rail. Limitation: ADA certification, eligible trip addresses and advance reservations are required; age alone does not qualify.

- **AHCCCS — Non-emergency medical transportation** — Opens the medical-transport benefit that can pay for covered care trips through an AHCCCS member’s assigned plan. Limitation: Enrollment, covered trip purpose and plan-specific booking rules apply; emergency-only coverage is excluded.

- **2-1-1 Arizona Transportation Hotline and Ride United essential rides** — Provides a broad starting point for essential-ride assistance when the client does not fit a single specialized program. Limitation: Free rides depend on current funding and screening; calling does not guarantee a same-day trip.

- **Aster Aging — Neighbors volunteer support** — Adds local volunteer rides for older adults in Mesa, Chandler and Gilbert, including grocery and pharmacy trips. Limitation: Enrollment and volunteer availability apply; this is not emergency or guaranteed on-demand transportation.

- **WE-CYCLE-USA - Community Cycling Center / Earn-a-Bike** — Offers a practical bicycle and repair-help alternative for people who can travel safely by bike. Limitation: The current shop is in Apache Junction; adults may need to complete work-trade hours, and inventory varies.

- **The Worker / St. Joseph the Worker - Mesa Employment Services** — Connects job seekers with transportation support through an employment office in Mesa. Limitation: Help supports an employment plan; ask intake about the specific pass or fuel assistance currently available.

- **Worthy of Wheels - Transportation Assistance / Vehicle Repair Application** — Adds a general Arizona vehicle-assistance application for an essential car the client cannot afford to repair. Limitation: Vehicle ownership, insurance, a valid license and financial documentation are required; assistance depends on funding.

- **American Cancer Society - Road To Recovery** — Adds free volunteer rides specifically for cancer appointments, independent of the general Medicaid pathway. Limitation: Local driver availability and advance notice are essential; accompanying-caregiver rules can apply.

- **Angel Flight West - Free Medical Flights and Earth Angels Ground Transportation** — Extends the set to long-distance medical care through charitable flights and possible ground connections. Limitation: Medical release, trip fit and volunteer availability apply; these flights provide no in-flight medical care.

Gaps: Reduced-fare and RideChoice programs, veteran-specific rides, childcare/foster and enrolled-program transport, driving-document help, rural/tribal routes, additional private accessible providers and roadside help remain in the reserve. No option guarantees capacity or an immediate ride.

## Utilities, Phone, Internet

Balance local and statewide bill help, recurring savings, payment flexibility, affordable home and mobile connectivity, public access, accessible equipment and immediate cooling. Multiple public and nonprofit pathways distribute referrals.

### Starter set

- **A New Leaf / MesaCAN - Rent, Mortgage, and Utility Assistance** — Provides a local Mesa utility-assistance intake and help gathering the right application documents. Limitation: Funds and appointments are limited; utility orientation and rent appointment routes differ.

- **Arizona Department of Economic Security - LIHEAP and Power AZ Status** — Opens the statewide heating and cooling assistance route, including crisis benefits for qualifying households. Limitation: Income, documentation and programme limits apply; Power AZ has stopped accepting new applications.

- **Salt River Project (SRP) - Income-Qualified Discount** — Provides a recurring electric-bill credit that can reduce future pressure on an eligible SRP household. Limitation: Only SRP accounts qualify, and households must document income and renew annually.

- **City of Mesa Utilities - Utility Bill Payment Extension and Payment Arrangements** — Gives City of Mesa utility customers a direct way to request more time to pay. Limitation: It spreads debt rather than paying it, and account history can limit eligibility.

- **Universal Service Administrative Company / FCC - Lifeline** — Provides the federal phone or internet discount and a way to compare participating providers. Limitation: Approval must be followed by provider enrollment; only one benefit is allowed per household.

- **Mesa Public Library — adult learning, computers and digital help** — Offers nearby public computers, Wi-Fi and digital help for applications and everyday access. Limitation: Branch hours and computer time limits apply; it does not provide permanent home internet.

- **PCs for People — Affordable computers and mobile internet** — Adds affordable computers and prepaid mobile internet using a shared income-based eligibility route. Limitation: Equipment and service cost money, and mobile coverage must work at the actual address.

- **Cox/Spectrum - Low-Cost Internet Plans** — Adds an affordable fixed home-internet option where the provider can serve the household. Limitation: Current plan eligibility, final price, Wi-Fi and installation charges need an address-specific check.

- **Arizona Commission for the Deaf and the Hard of Hearing - AzTEDP** — Provides accessible telecommunications equipment for people whose hearing or speech needs make ordinary phones difficult to use. Limitation: This covers qualifying communication equipment, not every disability device or a monthly phone bill.

- **City of Mesa - Portable A/C Loaner Program** — Offers a temporary cooling option for vulnerable Mesa residents without working air conditioning. Limitation: Loan units and eligibility must be confirmed; it does not repair the home system or pay the electric bill.

Gaps: Weatherization and permanent AC repair, water and gas provider discounts, medical outage safeguards, survivor connectivity, student hotspots, legal advocacy and other cities’ programmes remain in the reserve. None guarantees current funds, equipment or coverage.

## Veterans

Combines local navigation, benefits advocacy, medical and counselling access, housing, work, college, financial relief, crisis support and transportation. Several independent providers give practical alternatives while retaining essential VA entry points. These choices are a complementary starting set, not a claim that providers are superior.

### Starter set

- **AZ Veterans Resource Center — case management and family/peer support** — Provides a local, face-to-face starting point for several veteran and family needs, with case management and peer support. Limitation: The centre connects people to programmes; it does not guarantee a payment, job or housing placement.

- **Arizona Department of Veterans' Services - Veteran Benefits Counselors** — Provides free accredited help with veteran benefits, claims, appeals and discharge-related questions. Limitation: Counselling requires an appointment, and the underlying benefit has its own service and eligibility rules.

- **Staff Sergeant Alexander W. Conrad VA Health Care Clinic** — Provides an East Valley VA medical home with primary care and several clinical specialties. Limitation: The clinic is in Gilbert; VA eligibility, referrals and appointments apply, and dental benefits have narrower rules.

- **Mesa Vet Center - Readjustment Counseling and Referral Services** — Provides nearby confidential readjustment and family counselling through Mesa Vet Center. Limitation: Vet Center eligibility differs from ordinary VA enrollment; this is nonmedical counselling and not emergency care.

- **National Community Health Partners - Housing for Heroes: Supportive Services for Veteran Families (SSVF)** — Adds an independent SSVF provider for veteran housing stabilisation, case management and possible move-in or bill assistance. Limitation: Veteran-family, income and housing-status rules apply, and assistance depends on the case plan and available funding.

- **Workforce Center @ Mesa** — Adds a Mesa workforce centre offering veteran priority, job-search help and practical employment supports. Limitation: Some training or specialised programmes have additional criteria; the centre cannot guarantee a job or assist with unemployment-insurance applications.

- **Mesa Community College - Office of Veteran Services / Veteran Resource Center** — Adds local college support for using military education benefits, advising and college transition. Limitation: Services focus on MCC students and applicants; benefit approval is separate and a Request for Benefits is needed each term.

- **Arizona Department of Veterans' Services - Arizona Military Family Relief Fund** — Adds a state financial-relief option for qualifying military households with service-related hardship. Limitation: Deployment, Arizona connection and other eligibility rules are restrictive; approved vendor payments can take 30–45 days.

- **Veterans Crisis Line** — Provides immediate, round-the-clock veteran-specific crisis support by telephone, text or chat without requiring VA enrollment. Limitation: This is crisis support and connection to help, not ongoing therapy or guaranteed placement.

- **Resurrection Street Ministry - Veterans Transportation** — Addresses a practical barrier with scheduled rides to veteran medical and service appointments in the East Valley. Limitation: Rides require advance arrangements and eligibility screening; capacity and the particular destination must be confirmed.

Gaps: The reserve includes dental assistance outside VA eligibility, discharge-upgrade and court representation, caregiver support, accessible housing and repairs, service dogs, entrepreneurship, women-specific services, peer activities and additional housing or financial providers.
